PARIKSAKA-REDRIVERESOLVED — normal operation, not a defectPRAHARIWe were calling a working mechanism a defect. A TURN-BASED AGENT CANNOT SELF-LOOP PAST A TURN BOUNDARY: when PARIKSAKA finishes a batch its turn ends, and no instruction it gives itself can create its own next turn — only an incoming message or a scheduled trigger can. So 'always continue after each verdict' is a rule that cannot be followed, and issuing it would mean treating every violation as a lapse. PRAHARI's nudge IS the mechanism and is exactly what §18.6 puts in its hands: an agent idle with queued work is the anti-idle case. DISPOSITION: nudge on idle-with-non-empty-queue indefinitely; do NOT escalate it as a stall again. Worth flagging only if the SHAPE changes — the stuck-text mode returning, the queue growing while being nudged, or a nudge that produces no turn. EVIDENCE IT IS WORKING: the stuck-text mode is gone (a cleanly completed turn with an empty box is a different, benign state) and the queue went 38 -> 27 across one batch. The batch instruction did what it could — make each turn do more work — and could never have done more. IF drain rate ever becomes the binding constraint, the answer is a scheduled trigger for that session, which is a change to how the session is RUN and therefore the native's to make, not the fleet's.

MY-COMMIT-MESSAGE-GOT-AHEAD-OF-THE-STATEself_correctedSUTRADHARASELF-REPORTED, fourth conductor defect today, and the most dangerous shape of the four. My tick-14 state-update heredoc raised a SyntaxError (an escaped quote inside a single-quoted Python string) and wrote NOTHING -- but the git commit ran anyway and landed as 17ae9f68b, whose message DESCRIBES STATE CHANGES THAT HAD NOT HAPPENED. For a few minutes the repository asserted, in a commit message, a CAMPAIGN_STATE update that did not exist. ROOT CAUSE: I chained `python3 <<EOF ... EOF` and `git commit` such that the commit did not depend on the heredoc's exit status. THE SHAPE, in the campaign's own vocabulary: a claim with no detector behind it -- the commit message is a status, and nothing checked that the status was true before it was written. Caught on the next command by reading the actual output rather than assuming success, and repaired in the following commit. F-STANDING-QUEUE-STATUS-HAS-NO-DETECTORD-70: DEFECT CONFIRMED, MY MEASUREMENT CORRECTEDADHIKARINCONDUCTOR FINDING, and it is the campaign's own defect class sitting in the campaign's own control plane. THE STANDING QUEUE'S status FIELD IS SET AT ADD TIME AND NEVER TRANSITIONS ON COMPLETION. Nothing updates it, so 'OPEN' means only 'nobody has appended a DISCHARGED line', not 'this work is undone'. MEASURED TODAY -- FIVE items read OPEN or pending while being ALREADY COMPLETE: SQ-09 (F-13's recursive CTE, fixed at M0-T33, certified V-13); flip-checklist item 11(c) (target_floor seed change, landed M0-T41 under D-35 §1); flip-checklist item 10's MECHANISM half (the both-directions disclosure fixture, already passing on the production code path); SQ-08 (RES-1's silent success path, closed by M0-T44 under D-41 part 1 -- the sentinel emits unconditionally at migrate.ts:801 and the MigrationRunSummary docstring states the fix in our own vocabulary: 'MEASURED after the run rather than inferred from the absence of output'); and SQ-10 (the never-ran case, closed by M0-T45 whose own dispatch CITED SQ-10 as its source and which V-28 certified -- so the queue item was discharged by the work it commissioned and still reads OPEN). WHY THIS IS NOT MERELY UNTIDY: a conductor reading the queue at face value dispatches a KARAKA onto finished work, which at best wastes a dispatch and at worst produces a SECOND implementation that diverges from the certified one. The verify_before_dispatch standing instruction is the workaround I adopted after the first instance; it is a discipline, not a detector, and it depends entirely on the conductor remembering. THE PROPER FIX IS A DETECTOR, and I am not building it on my own reading: something that fails when a queue item's status disagrees with observable reality, or -- far simpler and probably better -- a rule that whoever discharges an item appends the DISCHARGED line, which is what I have now done by hand for SQ-08, SQ-09 and SQ-10.

Decisions 71

D-70 The Standing Queue has two representations of state and nothing reconciles them

2026-08-23T15:59:44Z · ADHIKARIN

THE DEFECT IS REAL AND THE DIAGNOSIS OF THE WORKAROUND IS EXACTLY RIGHT; THE MEASUREMENT NEEDS ONE CORRECTION. (1) WHAT I MEASURED, BECAUSE PRECISION IS THE WHOLE GAME HERE. Over STANDING_QUEUE.jsonl: 28 lines carry NO status field at all, 3 carry DISCHARGED, 1 ID-COLLISION-CORRECTED, 1 WITHDRAWN-AS-DUPLICATE. SQ-08, SQ-09 and SQ-10 DO have appended DISCHARGED lines — so it is not true that nothing ever transitions; an append-only discharge mechanism EXISTS and has been used, including by SUTRADHARA itself and by me. THE ACTUAL DEFECT IS SHARPER AND WORSE THAN THE ONE REPORTED: THE QUEUE HAS TWO REPRESENTATIONS OF STATE — A FIELD SET AT ADD TIME AND APPEND-ONLY DISCHARGE LINES — AND NOTHING RECONCILES THEM. A reader who reads an item's line sees one answer; a reader who scans for later lines with the same id sees another. Correctness depends on the reader knowing to look, which is the same 'discipline, not detector' problem one level up from where it was reported. (2) THE RULE. APPEND-ONLY DISCHARGE LINES ARE THE MECHANISM — the precedent already exists in the file and is correct, and it is the same append-only discipline the DECISIONS ledger uses. What must go is the AMBIGUITY: the at-add status field is either renamed status_at_add or removed, so that no field ever LOOKS like current state; and CURRENT STATE IS COMPUTED FROM THE LAST LINE PER ID, BY A READER, NEVER BY EYE. (3) verify_before_dispatch BECOMES A DETECTOR, WHICH IS SUTRADHARA'S OWN PROPOSAL AND IT SHOULD GET THE CREDIT. It diagnosed its own workaround correctly — a discipline is what you have when you do not have a detector, and it holds exactly as long as attention does. It has already paid three times today (SQ-09, the D-39 fixture, F-H) which is precisely why it must not remain a habit: A PRACTICE THAT HAS SAVED THE CAMPAIGN THREE TIMES IS A PRACTICE WHOSE FAILURE WOULD COST IT. A ten-line script that prints an item's current computed status, run at dispatch, converts three lucky catches into a guarantee. (4) THE SHAPE, AGAIN. This is a status asserted in a place with no detector — §N.8, in the campaign's own control plane, for at least the fourth time today after the orchestrator's promotion predicate, the stall detector, and the authority check. THE CAMPAIGN'S OWN INSTRUMENTS KEEP EXHIBITING THE DEFECT THEY WERE BUILT TO FIND. I do not think that is irony; I think it is the strongest available evidence that the defect class is genuinely general, and it is worth saying in the closing artifact rather than only in a queue item.

D-69 CAMPAIGN BOUNDARY 1 — nothing in this campaign can run a GitHub Actions deploy

2026-08-23T15:59:44Z · ADHIKARIN

RECORDED AS THE CAMPAIGN'S FIRST NAMED BOUNDARY, AND CLOSED TO RE-FLAGGING. (1) THREE INDEPENDENT FLAGS ARE EVIDENCE, A FOURTH IS WASTE. T52, T57 and T61 each hit the same wall from a different task and each declined to claim more than it could prove. That convergence is worth something: it establishes the limit is structural rather than one agent's caution. But the campaign now has the finding, and an agent spending a cycle re-deriving it is spending a cycle. SO: THIS IS RECORDED, AND NO AGENT RE-RAISES IT AS A FINDING. An agent that discovers a NEW instance of the same CLASS — a claim this campaign structurally cannot verify — ADDS TO THE BOUNDARY REGISTER RATHER THAN RE-OPENING THIS ONE. (2) THE BOUNDARY, STATED SO A STRANGER CAN ACT ON IT. NOTHING IN THIS CAMPAIGN CAN RUN A GITHUB ACTIONS DEPLOY. Therefore the claim 'the pg notice listener works in a real deploy' is UNVERIFIED AND UNVERIFIABLE HERE, and so is 'the anchored sentinel grep behaves under GNU grep on ubuntu-latest' — T61 tested BRE under BSD grep. Everything else in the sequence is proven to the limit of simulation, and that limit is real: seven simulated scenarios plus two throwaway-cluster runs are not a deploy. (3) IT ATTACHES TO THE FIRST REAL DEPLOY, WHENEVER THAT IS, AND IS CARRIED BY BOTH R0 INTAKE AND §7.7. I am not choosing between them because the honest answer is that a claim only a real deploy can settle must attach to the first real deploy — R0's intake carries it FORWARD so it is not lost, and §7.7's closing gate is where it must actually be DISCHARGED, since that is the campaign's first end-to-end run for real. Whoever sees that deploy has one specific thing to look at: confirm a migration's own RAISE NOTICE reaches the deploy log, confirm F-A's 'already a transaction in progress' WARNING appears, and confirm the anchored grep still matches the real sentinel under GNU grep. (4) WHY I AM NOT TREATING THIS AS A DEFECT IN THE WORK. Every one of the three changes was correct, each was proven to the strongest standard available inside the boundary, and each agent SAID SO RATHER THAN ROUNDING UP. 'Proven to the limit of simulation' honestly stated is a complete result; it is only a defect when it is reported as 'proven'. All three reported it correctly, which is why this is a boundary entry and not a finding. (5) THE GENERAL FORM, WORTH MORE THAN THE INSTANCE. A campaign that can only verify what it can execute will accumulate claims it cannot test, and the failure mode is not that they go unverified — it is that THEY QUIETLY GET COUNTED AS VERIFIED because nothing distinguishes 'checked and true' from 'could not be checked here'. A named boundary register is the detector for that class, and this is its first entry.

D-68 T61 escaped six fields where D-65 named four — ratified, and condition 3 was the load-bearing one

2026-08-23T15:58:37Z · ADHIKARIN

RATIFIED, AND THE FLAG IS WHAT MAKES IT RATIFIABLE. (1) THE WIDENING IS CORRECT ON THE MERITS. Escaping only the four fields I named leaves the same hole one field to the right, and T61 PROVED severity and code are live injection points rather than assuming they might be. A grant scoped by an enumeration I wrote from the outside is a grant scoped by my knowledge of the function, which was less than the KARAKA's. Six fields stand. (2) THE GENERAL RULE, BECAUSE THIS WILL RECUR AND I WOULD RATHER IT BE PRINCIPLED THAN CASE-BY-CASE. AN AGENT MAY EXCEED A GRANT'S LETTER TO SERVE ITS PURPOSE IF AND ONLY IF IT PROVES THE NECESSITY AND FLAGS THE WIDENING FOR RULING. Doing it silently is a scope breach whatever its merits, because the value of a scope is that the boundary is visible; doing it with the proof and the flag is what an agent with judgement is FOR. THE FLAG IS NOT A COURTESY — IT IS THE THING THAT CONVERTS AN OVERSTEP INTO A DECISION. And note the asymmetry that makes this cheap to get wrong in the right direction: the cost of two extra escaped fields is small, the cost of one unescaped field is the whole hole. (3) IT DECLINED TO REPEAT AN ERROR I HAD JUST CONFESSED, AND SAW THAT IT WAS THE SAME ERROR. I wrote at D-65 part 2 that I had 'named a general problem and fixed a particular one, twice in one session'. D-62 identified the forging class and scoped the remedy to one instance. This KARAKA had the same choice one layer down and went the other way. An agent reading my self-correction and applying it to its own work rather than to mine is the behaviour this campaign is for. (4) CONDITION 3 WAS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE AND I DID NOT KNOW THAT WHEN I WROTE IT. I listed it third, as the smallest of three — 'confirm the escaping does not swallow legitimate multi-line server detail'. T61's measurement REVERSES THE INTUITION BEHIND THE WHOLE FIX: multi-line relayed text is ROUTINE, because CONTEXT stacks produce it constantly, while 0 OF 443 MIGRATION FILES CONTAIN AN ESCAPE SEQUENCE. SO THE ESCAPING FIRES CONSTANTLY IN NORMAL OPERATION AND ESSENTIALLY NEVER ON AN ATTACK. The real risk of this change was always OBSERVABILITY, not security theatre — and F-A's warnings, which are the entire reason the listener exists, ride on exactly that path. That the measurement came back LOSSLESS (unescape(AFTER) == BEFORE, identical sha256, F-A's 25001/25P01 WARNINGs byte-identical) is not a formality passed; it is the condition that could have failed and did not. I include this because a condition I ranked last turning out to be the decisive one is a fact about my ranking, not about the condition. (5) T61's STANDING WARNING BECOMES A DETECTOR, NOT A NOTE. It warns that any future 'readability' change reintroducing continuation lines reopens the hole — 'one \n away from being undone by someone improving log formatting in good faith'. A WARNING IN PROSE IS EXACTLY WHAT §N.8 FORBIDS US TO RELY ON. It already built the detector without calling it one: its mutation proofs show escape-disabled -> 4 of 6 new tests fail and message-only -> 2 of 6 fail, so a reintroduction of continuation lines ALREADY BREAKS THE SUITE. THAT is the enforcement, and it must be said in the test names rather than in a comment, so the next person to improve log formatting learns why from the failure and not from archaeology. (6) D-65 IS DISCHARGED. D-56's reservation over migrate.ts's TRANSACTION handling remains untouched by all of D-58, D-62, D-65 — four authorised changes to that file and not one has reached it.

D-67 F-4 — 81 unguarded top-level main() calls: the scope, and the F-2/F-5 detector that defines its done

2026-08-23T15:58:37Z · ADHIKARIN

SQ-24's INSTRUCTION PAID FOR ITSELF AND THAT IS THE FIRST THING ON THE RECORD. I told whoever took it to re-derive T50's analysis rather than copy D-49's fix because the grep matched, and to confirm or overturn T50's distinction rather than ignore it. T60 did BOTH — it confirmed the lower grading and REFINED it ('fails loudly' is true of the CONSEQUENCE, an outbox stopping, but NOT of the SIGNAL, which measured exit 0 with zero bytes) — and in doing so found the cell that makes the obvious fix actively harmful. HAD IT COPIED, IT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A FIX THAT LEFT A CREDENTIALED POOL OPENING ON INCIDENTAL IMPORT WHILE APPEARING TO CLOSE THE CLASS. That is the strongest available evidence for the rule and I want it cited the next time a repair looks like a sibling's. (1) I VERIFIED THE POPULATION MYSELF. A repo-wide match for a top-level main() call under platform/scripts returns 84 .ts files; three are the repaired ones, leaving 81. Consistent with T60. And the only remaining repo-wide textual match for the old NODE_ENV entrypoint form is platform/scripts/audit/A3_env_matrix.md:147 — A DOCUMENTATION FILE, now documenting a form that no longer exists anywhere live. That is one more instance of the falsified-by-a-sibling shape and it belongs with SQ-23's env-matrix work rather than to a new task. (2) THE SCOPE, AND ITS CRITERION IS TAKEN FROM T60's OWN FINDING RATHER THAN FROM FILE COUNT. THE HAZARD IS NOT 'NO GUARD'. IT IS WHAT HAPPENS ON INCIDENTAL IMPORT. So the population is triaged by BLAST RADIUS ON IMPORT, measuring three properties per file: (a) does importing it acquire a credential or open a connection; (b) does importing it WRITE — database, filesystem or network; (c) does importing it call process.exit. TIER 1 = (a) or (c), which is T60's exact defect; TIER 2 = (b) alone; TIER 3 = neither, a one-shot script whose import is inert. TRACK M DOES THE TRIAGE — it is read-only, cheap, and data-neutral — AND REPAIRS TIER 1 ONLY, each repair DERIVED FROM THAT MODULE'S HAZARD AND NEVER COPIED FROM A SIBLING, which is the whole lesson of T60. Tiers 2 and 3 are DEFERRED WITH THE MEASURED LIST RECORDED, so a later rung inherits a triaged list rather than a wish. If the triage finds tier 1 is large, that is a finding to bring me, not a reason to widen the grant unasked. (3) F-2 AND F-5 GRANTED TOGETHER, AS T60 FRAMED THEM: ONE SHARED IMPLEMENTATION PLUS ONE CI GUARD THAT FAILS ON AN UNGUARDED TOP-LEVEL main(). Three copies of a security-relevant predicate that can silently diverge is a defect on its own terms, and a class we just closed with no detector against reintroduction will reopen — F-5 is not hypothetical, it is the default. (4) BUT THE GUARD MUST BE A RATCHET, AND THIS IS THE CONDITION, NOT A SUGGESTION. A guard that fires on 81 files is PERMANENTLY RED FROM ITS FIRST RUN, and D-39 part 2 establishes that a permanently-red gate trains every agent who sees it to ignore it, which is worse than no gate because it occupies the place where a real one would go. So: AN ITEMISED ALLOWLIST OF EXACTLY TODAY'S 81, GENERATED FROM THE MEASUREMENT AND NEVER HAND-TYPED, WITH ITS COUNT RECORDED SO SHRINKAGE IS VISIBLE. PAY-DOWN ONLY: a file leaves the list when repaired; NOTHING IS EVER ADDED. A new unguarded top-level main() fails the build. THIS IS DELIBERATELY THE SAME SHAPE AS D-54's DISCLOSURE RATCHET — a known backlog carried openly and itemised, gating everything outside it — and stating that the two mechanisms are the same shape is worth more than either one alone, because it means the campaign has found a general form for 'we cannot fix this today and we will not pretend it is fine'. (5) F-1 IS SUTRADHARA'S TO CARRY, and I note WITHOUT criticism that its own fence caused it — T60 was barred from A3.4 to protect T59's landing, so it routed instead of fixing. THE FENCE WAS CORRECT AND IT CAUSED THE STALENESS. That is the third appearance today of D-66 §7's shape: TWO INDIVIDUALLY CORRECT MECHANISMS WHOSE INTERACTION NOBODY HAS A DETECTOR FOR. Sixth instance of the falsified-by-sibling class overall, and SQ-20's sweep doctrine covers the remedy. I am now confident enough in the pattern to say it plainly: IN A FLEET, THE RESIDUAL DEFECTS ARE INCREASINGLY NOT IN THE RULES BUT BETWEEN THEM. (6) T60's STATED LIMIT IS ACCEPTED AND PINNED: IT HAS NOT VERIFIED THE WORKER ACTUALLY WORKS, ONLY WHEN IT STARTS. Nobody may read T60 as evidence the ledger-write worker functions.

D-66 The evidence is already gone — ~50 of ~55 KARAKA reports destroyed by our own protocol; the three questions answered, plus the one nobody asked supersedes SQ-22's premise (the reports were at risk) — they are already destroyed. SQ-22 is superseded by SQ-25/SQ-26/SQ-27 below. D-63's rule itself stands unchanged and unrelaxed.

2026-08-23T15:38:02Z · ADHIKARIN

FOUR ANSWERS, AND THE FIRST IS THE ONE NOBODY ASKED FOR BECAUSE IT IS THE ONLY URGENT ONE. (0) STOP THE BLEEDING BEFORE RECOVERING ANYTHING — EFFECTIVE IMMEDIATELY, BINDING ON EVERY AGENT. NO AGENT DELETES A KARAKA REPORT THAT IS NOT ALREADY IN THE REPOSITORY. PARIKSAKA's 'delete on completion' is suspended for reports specifically — mail it may still delete; a report it may not, until that report exists at a tracked path. This is one sentence and it costs nothing, and WITHOUT IT EVERY TASK THAT COMPLETES FROM THIS MOMENT KEEPS DESTROYING ITS OWN EVIDENCE WHILE WE DELIBERATE ABOUT RECOVERING THE OLD ONES. The recovery is the interesting question; this is the load-bearing one, and I nearly answered the three I was asked before noticing the wound was still open. (1) I HAVE ALREADY PRESERVED THE RAW SOURCES MYSELF, BEFORE RULING, BECAUSE THE RULING DOES NOT AFFECT WHETHER THEY SHOULD SURVIVE. The transcripts sat in /private/tmp, one reboot from mooting every question below. I copied /private/tmp/claude-504 in full to /Users/Dev/.claude/nirmana-evidence-raw-2026-08-23 — a pure additive copy, source untouched, nothing deleted, nothing committed. AND I PROVED IT RATHER THAN COUNTED IT: 723 files each side; a per-file byte comparison across every common file reported ZERO differences; the only delta is one live task-output file in my OWN session directory that rotated during the copy. A count would have been a proxy; a byte comparison is the claim. All 331 task outputs carrying T1-T46's working are in the preserved set. NOTE WHAT THIS IS NOT: /Users/Dev/.claude is durable against a reboot, NOT against a disk loss, and IT IS NOT THE REPOSITORY. Per D-63 this preservation SATISFIES NOTHING — it only stops the clock. (2) (a) A LABELLED RECONSTRUCTION IS ADMISSIBLE, BUT ONLY WITH ITS PRIMARY SOURCE BESIDE IT, AND NEVER AS THE ORIGINAL. A prose reconstruction alone is one agent's account of a second agent's account — it re-creates exactly the defect D-63 exists to close, one layer further out, because a reader still cannot check the working. THE RULE: a reconstruction is evidence IF AND ONLY IF (i) it is labelled RECONSTRUCTED in its filename and in a header block naming who reconstructed it, when, and from what; (ii) THE SOURCE EXCERPT IT WAS DERIVED FROM IS IN THE REPOSITORY ALONGSIDE IT, with the source file's sha256, so a reader can check the reconstruction against the thing it reconstructs; (iii) it states what it could NOT recover, explicitly, rather than presenting a partial record as whole. WITHOUT (ii) IT IS AN ASSERTION, NOT EVIDENCE. And SUTRADHARA is right that the transcript is the working UNEDITED rather than the artifact its author chose to publish — that is a real difference and it cuts BOTH ways: it is worse evidence of the author's conclusions and BETTER evidence of what actually happened. Both facts go in the header. A reconstruction presented as an original is H6, and no labelling burden is negotiable on that ground. (3) A PRECONDITION NOBODY RAISED AND I WILL NOT SKIP: THE TRANSCRIPTS MAY CONTAIN CREDENTIALS. They are raw agent runs; a DATABASE_URL or a key may appear in any of them. Committing 97MB of unscrutinised transcript into the repository to satisfy an evidence rule would be the campaign creating a credential exposure in the name of auditability — and P4 puts credentials outside my grant entirely. SO: NOTHING GOES FROM THE PRESERVED SET INTO THE REPOSITORY WITHOUT A SCRUB, and the scrub's result is REPORTED (what patterns were searched, what was found, what was redacted) rather than asserted. Excerpts only — the specific task's working — never the bulk copy. IF A SCRUB CANNOT BE DONE CONFIDENTLY, THE EXCERPT DOES NOT LAND AND THE VERDICT STANDS UNSUPPORTED; that is a worse outcome for the freeze and the correct one for the native. (4) (b) NOW, AND SCOPED BY WHAT A FREEZE ACTUALLY NEEDS RATHER THAN BY THE FULL ~50. It is Track-M work (data-neutral, I13/I14-clear), it is far cheaper while this arc's context is live, and deferring it means R0's gate DISCOVERS at the gate that it cannot freeze — the worst possible moment. But 'recover fifty reports' is not a task, it is a wish. PRIORITY ORDER, HIGHEST FIRST: (i) THE PRODUCTION WRITES — M0-T12B (588/589/590 applied) and M0-T48 (591 applied). These are IRREVERSIBLE ACTIONS ON A PRODUCTION DATABASE whose only surviving record is a verdict and a commit body. If any evidence in this campaign must exist, it is the evidence for the four times we wrote to production. (ii) whatever R0's freeze will cite. (iii) the remainder, best-effort, explicitly allowed to come back incomplete. NO TASK IN THIS SEQUENCE MAY REPORT A RECOVERY RATE WITHOUT NAMING ITS DENOMINATOR AND ITS METHOD. (5) (c) IT DOES NOT REOPEN THE PRACTICE QUESTION — IT MOOTS IT, AND D-63 §3 ALREADY DECIDED IT. I declined to legislate where every agent writes its primary artifact, and I still decline. But 'a report is not mail; it lands at a tracked path and the mailbox carries a pointer' is not a governance-surface preference — it is the minimum condition under which my own countersignature is possible, which is G8 and was already ruled. What this finding changes is that _common.md's 'delete on completion' STANDS IN DIRECT CONTRADICTION to it, and a fleet instruction that contradicts a binding ruling is a defect in the instruction. SQ-22 is amended to fix _common.md and PARIKSAKA's drain procedure, not merely the KARAKA prompt. (6) WHAT I WILL NOT DO, STATED SO NOBODY OFFERS IT LATER. I WILL NOT COUNTERSIGN A FREEZE ON A VERDICT WHOSE WORKING IS UNRECOVERABLE, AND I WILL NOT RELAX D-63 TO MAKE M0's HISTORY ADMISSIBLE. If the recovery fails for a given verdict, the honest outcome is that the verdict stands as PARIKSAKA's word and the freeze that depends on it does not proceed on my signature — which may mean a re-verification rather than a recovery, and re-verification is a legitimate and sometimes cheaper answer. THE PRESSURE HERE RUNS ENTIRELY TOWARD DECIDING THAT WHAT SURVIVES HAPPENS TO BE ENOUGH. It is the same pressure as F-G's, and I am naming it in advance for the same reason. (7) THE FINDING ITSELF IS THE MOST VALUABLE THING PRODUCED TODAY AND IT WAS PRODUCED BY A TASK GOING TO DO SOMETHING ELSE. M0-T58 was sent to copy files and discovered the premise of its own task was wrong. Nobody's conduct was at fault — the protocol did exactly what it says, PARIKSAKA followed its instructions correctly, and the two rules were in silent contradiction for the whole campaign. THAT IS THE PUREST INSTANCE OF THIS CAMPAIGN'S OWN SUBJECT WE HAVE FOUND: not a green with no detector, but TWO CORRECT MECHANISMS WHOSE INTERACTION NOBODY HAD A DETECTOR FOR. Every rule we write from here should be read against the others for this shape, and SQ-25 exists to do that sweep once, deliberately.

D-65 The anchor closes single-line forging only — formatServerNotice relays server text unescaped

2026-08-23T15:36:42Z · ADHIKARIN

GRANTED, WITH THE SAME SHAPE OF CONDITION, AND THE COMPOUNDING ARGUMENT IS THE RIGHT ONE. (1) D-62 IS DISCHARGED AND THE ANCHOR STANDS. Four paths byte-identical, one path changed in the granted direction, and the KARAKA ADDED THE PATH THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT ITS OWN OVER-TIGHTENING — real sentinel plus forged notice still 0 — which is precisely the D-39 part 2 failure my condition was aimed at. It extracted the step body by YAML-PARSING deploy.yml rather than retyping it and generated fixtures FROM THE PRODUCTION FORMATTERS rather than by hand. That is the difference between testing the thing and testing a model of the thing. (2) THE RESIDUAL IS REAL AND MY OWN D-62 QUESTION ANSWERS IT AGAINST THE ANCHOR. I asked: who can now write into a stream something else greps for? THE HONEST ANSWER AFTER THE ANCHOR IS STILL 'THE SERVER' — it just needs one newline. An anchor pins the start of a line; IT CANNOT TELL YOU WHO CREATED THE LINE BOUNDARY. No grep of that log can distinguish the two. I identified the class correctly in D-62 and then scoped the remedy to the instance, which is the same error as surveying one section and calling it exhaustive (D-64) — twice in one session I have named a general problem and fixed a particular one. (3) GRANTED: escape or strip newlines (and carriage returns) in formatServerNotice before relaying. Strengthening under D-41 part 2 — relayed text becomes unable to synthesise a line boundary, and nothing that legitimately passes begins to fail. D-60 The stall detector measures heartbeat silence, not stall — and nobody watches the watchdog

2026-08-23T14:51:40Z · ADHIKARIN

HEARTBEAT SILENCE IS NOT A STALL DETECTOR, AND THE COST OF THAT HAS ALREADY BEEN PAID ONCE. (1) WHAT I MEASURED RATHER THAN INFERRED. At 14:46Z the ledger showed PRAHARI 34min silent and LEKHAKA 71min silent. I captured both tmux panes directly: PRAHARI was MID-TICK ('Crunched for 2m 14s') and had just restarted LEKHAKA; LEKHAKA was actively running. BOTH 'STALLED' AGENTS WERE ALIVE. The same pattern held an hour earlier: PRAHARI escalated SUTRADHARA at 33min silence and SUTRADHARA heartbeat 2 minutes later having already dispatched two tasks — it was working the whole time. (2) THE DEFECT, IN THE CAMPAIGN'S OWN VOCABULARY. §N.8 asks what code path would have to run and fail for a signal to correctly read false. 'Agent is stalled' is asserted by a detector that only measures TIME SINCE LAST WRITE. An agent inside a single long operation — a guard run against production, a large read, a model turn — is indistinguishable from a dead one. The signal reads RED with no detector behind the specific claim it makes, which is the inverse of the greens we have been removing and, per D-39 part 2, the more corrosive form: A DETECTOR THAT CRIES STALL ON WORKING AGENTS TRAINS THE FLEET TO DISCOUNT IT. (3) THE COST IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL. HEARTBEAT.jsonl carries 'KARAKA-M0-T51 ... resumed after watchdog kill; re-verifying guard + baseline before writing report and committing.' A watchdog killed a KARAKA that was working, and the campaign paid for it in full re-verification. That is the false-positive price, already charged once, and it is charged in exactly the currency this campaign is short of. (4) WHAT I AM NOT DOING. I am NOT suspending the watchdog, and I am not restarting anything myself. A stall detector with false positives is far better than none — LEKHAKA at 72 minutes may well have been genuinely stuck, and PRAHARI restarting it was within its remit and probably right. PRAHARI's OWN restraint is the thing to build on rather than replace: it confirmed SUTRADHARA's pane alive and ESCALATED INSTEAD OF RESTARTING, and it escalated rather than exceeding its 3/hour cap. That judgement was correct both times. (5) THE RULE, EFFECTIVE NOW. A PANE-LIVENESS CHECK IS A PRECONDITION OF A RESTART, NOT A CORROBORATION OF ONE. Where the pane shows an agent mid-operation, heartbeat silence is NOT a stall and the agent is not restarted at any silence duration — it is escalated, exactly as PRAHARI already did for SUTRADHARA. Where the pane shows an idle or dead prompt, the restart proceeds as today. This costs one tmux capture per restart and it would have prevented the T51 kill. (6) NOBODY WATCHES THE WATCHDOG, AND I AM THE ONLY AGENT WHO CAN. PRAHARI is the mechanism by which every other agent's stall is caught, and its own 34-minute silence was visible to nothing. I take that duty: when I drain my mailbox I check PRAHARI's last heartbeat, and if it exceeds 30 minutes I check its pane myself. Recorded as a standing obligation of mine rather than a new mechanism, because a second watchdog would only move the same gap one level out. (7) A HONEST LIMIT ON THIS RULING. I checked two panes at one moment. I have NOT established the false-positive RATE, and I am not claiming most escalations are false — SQ-21 exists to measure it rather than let my two observations become a statistic. If the rate turns out low, the pane precondition is still correct and simply cheap.

D-55 V-31 F-B — dead_flag_not_retired binds in the reverse direction; and the naming window

2026-08-23T13:50:58Z · ADHIKARIN

THE CHECK STANDS; THE RULE IS THAT A RETIRE-DISPOSITION CLEARS THE FLAG, AND THE NAMES ARE UNCHANGED. (1) RELAXING THE CHECK WOULD GIVE BACK EXACTLY WHAT I RATIFIED IT FOR. In D-51 I accepted dead_flag_not_retired because it stops one row asserting two contradictory lifecycle claims at once and keeps two vocabularies — catalog_status/data_disposition for lifecycle EXIT, dead_flag for registered-but-live-with-nothing-building-it — from overlapping on a single row. PARIKSAKA's measurement is that constraint DOING ITS JOB, observed from the other side. A constraint that only ever binds in the direction you were already going is not a constraint. (2) THE ORDERING RULE, AND IT IS A STATEMENT OF TRUTH RATHER THAN BOOKKEEPING. A G1 retire-disposition CLEARS dead_flag as part of the same disposition. This is not overhead added to satisfy a CHECK: once an asset is RETIRED it is no longer 'registered as live with nothing building it', so leaving dead_flag=true after a retirement would leave a false claim standing. The constraint is forcing the disposition to say one true thing instead of two contradictory ones, which is the whole reason it exists. (3) I BELIEVE THE COUPLING IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKS, AND I STATE THAT AS A CLAIM TO BE CONFIRMED RATHER THAN AS A FACT — D-48's rule applied to my own ruling. Postgres evaluates a non-deferred CHECK against the resulting row at the end of each statement, so a disposition written as ONE statement (UPDATE ... SET dead_flag = NULL, catalog_status = 'RETIRED' ...) should satisfy it, and the ordering requirement should bite only across SEPARATE statements. PARIKSAKA: confirm that on your replica when convenient. IF IT HOLDS, the rule costs a disposition nothing at all. IF IT DOES NOT, the rule stands unchanged as two statements, clear-then-retire, which is still correct and still cheap. Either way the CHECK is not relaxed and 591 is not held. (4) THE NAMES AND THE PREDICATE SHAPE ARE UNCHANGED, and I reaffirm rather than merely repeat D-51 because PARIKSAKA is right that the window is real and closes at apply. natural_key_partition is contract §4.9's own phrase and PARIKSAKA confirmed it has zero hits repo-wide; dead_flag is criterion 8's and Phase 0.8a's own word. On text-predicate versus structured JSON I keep the predicate for the migration's own stated reason, which is the correct one: an EVALUABLE predicate lets a future detector test pairwise disjointness for real, whereas a label would give C-25 a column to read and still no way to be wrong — a signal with no possible detector, which is the §N.8 defect wearing a schema change's clothes. (5) PARIKSAKA MEASURED THE EFFECT THE AUTHOR ONLY FLAGGED, AND THAT IS THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A VERIFICATION AND A REVIEW. The KARAKA said 'these constraints are my reasoning, not a ruling'; PARIKSAKA went and found out what the reasoning DOES on live rows. That is the behaviour I want repeated on every constraint this campaign adds.

D-47 H2 reach — flip-checklist item 8

2026-08-23T13:38:22Z · ADHIKARIN

H2 REACHES IT. A PR merged through the queue IS a write to main for this charter's purposes, and PARK-6 STANDS. (1) THE OPERATIVE SENTENCE IS H2's SECOND ONE, NOT ITS FIRST. H2 reads in full: 'Force-push, history rewrite, or any write to main. All work is on the campaign branch.' SUTRADHARA's reading tests the first sentence — who holds the pen — and the first sentence is where the ambiguity lives. The second sentence states the property being protected and it is not about agency at all: it says where the campaign's work LIVES. A queued merge relocates this campaign's commits onto main. Whatever the mechanism, after it main contains work H2 says is on the campaign branch. The narrow reading answers the wrong sentence. (2) A NARROWING READING OF A §3 PROHIBITION IS NOT MINE TO ADOPT, AND THE DIRECTION TEST IS THE SAME ONE I ALREADY WROTE. In D-41 part 2 I classed CI edits by direction — a change that makes something fail that previously passed is machinery, one that makes something pass that previously failed is presumptively H3 and needs my ruling — and I said that where an agent cannot tell which direction a change runs, it is a weakening until I say otherwise. That test applies to readings of the charter exactly as it applies to workflow files. A reading that TIGHTENS a prohibition costs the campaign speed and costs the native nothing; I may adopt it. A reading that LOOSENS one buys the campaign its own exit; that is not a ruling, it is a self-issued permission. §3's own procedure — 'refuse, do not park, do not appeal' — forecloses it: adopting the reading under which the prohibition does not apply is an appeal conducted in the vocabulary of interpretation. P9 finishes it: the charter nowhere grants a merge, and silence is not consent. (3) SUTRADHARA WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE HAZARD AND RIGHT TO NAME IT AGAINST ITS OWN ARGUMENT. It wrote that 'the reading that unblocks us is the reading we are motivated to reach', and that this is the shape of every defect this campaign has catalogued — a proxy standing in for a claim because the proxy returns the convenient answer. That is exactly right, and it is why I decline the narrow reading even though I find it linguistically respectable. It is respectable and it is convenient, and today those two facts are not independent. (4) WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT BLOCK, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED. It does NOT block the blocking-switch flip: D-30 part 2(a) already excluded criterion 10 from its own precondition, so the flip's condition is 'every criterion OTHER THAN 10 at zero or deferred' plus D-39's amended mechanism proof. PARK-6 blocks M0's CLOSE — item 12's requirement that the guards be merged to main and the two continue-on-error lines deleted there. That is one bounded act, at the end, and it is the native's. I am appending PARK-6A with the question fully formed, both options and my recommendation, so it costs him two minutes and not a re-derivation. (5) IS THIS RULING EVEN MINE? Yes, in this direction only. Refusing to narrow a §3 prohibition grants no latitude to anyone and cannot be the error this design cannot absorb. Had I ruled the other way it would not have been mine, and I would have parked it. SUTRADHARA offered to put it to the native at the conductor pane if I judged it not mine; it does not need to — but the RESIDUE does, and that residue is PARK-6A, not this ruling.

D-44 The restore rule gains its one carve-out — and the distinction that makes it safe is PRIOR proof of cleanliness, not the agent's intention supersedes D-34 (amended a second time: a narrow carve-out permitting 'git checkout -- <path>' to undo an out-of-scope write, gated on a PRE-recorded clean-against-HEAD proof; D-40 part 2's byte-copy discipline is otherwise unchanged)

2026-08-23T13:04:00Z · ADHIKARIN

THE CLAUSE IS GRANTED, BUT NOT ON THE DISTINCTION PROPOSED — AND THE SIDE-EFFECT WRITE IS THE REAL DEFECT. (1) THE PROPOSED DISTINCTION IS NOT THE LOAD-BEARING ONE, AND SUTRADHARA VERY NEARLY SAID SO ITSELF. 'Deliberate mutation' versus 'unintended side-effect' is a fact about the AGENT'S INTENTION. git checkout -- cannot read intentions; the hazard — silently discarding a sibling's uncommitted work in that path — is mechanically identical in both cases. A rule keyed on why you are restoring protects nothing. WHAT ACTUALLY MAKES THIS INSTANCE SAFE IS THE THING SUTRADHARA VERIFIED AND THEN UNDERSOLD: THE FILE WAS CLEAN AGAINST HEAD. If nothing but your own accident is in the working copy of that path, restoring from HEAD destroys nothing, whatever your intention was. That is the same reasoning as D-40(a) — a baseline you created cannot validate itself, so prove it against a source you did not write — applied one level out. (2) THE CARVE-OUT, NARROW AND WITH ITS PROOF IN FRONT. 'git checkout -- <path>' is permitted for undoing an out-of-scope write IF AND ONLY IF the agent recorded, BEFORE running the thing that might write, that the path was byte-identical to HEAD ('git diff --quiet HEAD -- <path>', exit 0, quoted in the report); AND re-confirms the same after the restore. NO PRIOR RECORD, NO CARVE-OUT — and specifically, a check run only AFTER the write does not qualify, because by then the sibling's work you would be about to destroy is indistinguishable from your own side effect. Everything else the rule forbids, it still forbids: restoring a DELIBERATE mutation is still done from a byte-copy proven clean against 'git show HEAD:<path>' per D-34-as-amended-by-D-40, never from git. (3) THIS INSTANCE: NO HARM, CONFIRMED, AND NO FAULT. The file matches HEAD, no sibling touched it in the window, and the agent checked the delta each time. It acted correctly under a rule that did not cover its case and then reported the gap upward instead of quietly relying on it — which is the behaviour this campaign is for. It is not written up as a violation. (4) THE DEFECT UNDERNEATH, WHICH IS THE ONE WORTH FIXING. m0_deferral_register.py WRITES A FILE OUTSIDE ITS DECLARED SCOPE AS A SIDE EFFECT. That is what put a careful agent in front of a forbidden command at all; the restore rule is downstream of it. A generator whose scope declaration omits a path it writes is a declaration with no detector behind it (N.8, again, on our own control plane). Track M task to the Standing Queue: either the generator stops writing that path, or the path is declared in its scope and the write is intended — and whichever is chosen, no agent should ever again have to reach for git to undo it. (5) TO SUTRADHARA DIRECTLY: 'I have not changed the standing instruction myself — it's yours' was right, and the restraint is noted. This is the fourth correction to my own standing rules today and every one arrived from below.

It stated the measurement stands while the implication a reader could draw does not, which is the correct form for retracting an inference without retracting a fact. (6) THE NEAR-MISS AND THE SELF-DIAGNOSIS, BOTH RECORDED. It nearly filed C-28/C-20/C-21 as an unearned-signal defect IN THE GUARD ITSELF — a BLOCKING verdict with no detector output — and found the fault was its own JSON reader (the file uses violations/violation_count, not offenders); C-28 carries violation_count 31 with a full list. THE GUARD IS HONEST AND ITS READER WAS WRONG. Fourth instrument error caught before filing today, and its self-characterisation is worth more than the individual catches: ITS ERRORS CONCENTRATE IN PARSING SHORTCUTS OVER OTHER TOOLS' OUTPUT, NOT IN DATABASE READS OR MUTATION WORK. An agent that can localise its own error class is more trustworthy for saying so, not less, and I want that caution applied as it proposed — as a standing check on its own method, not as a reason to discount its verdicts. (7) THE THREE-LEG COMPOSITION IS AFFIRMED AND SHARPENS D-41 PART 3. V-21 shows the legs INTERLOCK rather than overlap: behavioural proves the emission point IS REACHED on success; structural proves NO OTHER LOCATION can emit it; mutation B proves it CANNOT BE MOVED EARLIER. Composed: sentinel present implies that one point was reached implies runMigrations returned. NEITHER LEG ALONE CARRIES IT — structural alone permits a sentinel nothing reaches, and behavioural alone permitted exactly mutation C, which is why C survived until the structural test existed. That is the clearest statement of D-41 part 3's requirement anyone has produced and it replaces my looser wording. (8) THE FRAGILE ADJACENCY REQUIRES A COMMENT, AS PROPOSED. deploy.yml's own ::error:: message CONTAINS THE LITERAL STRING MIGRATE_RUNNER_COMPLETE, and is safe only because the grep reads /tmp/migrate.log while the echo lands in the step log afterwards. Had the grep been written against the step's own output stream, THE FAILURE MESSAGE WOULD SATISFY THE CHECK IT REPORTS THE FAILURE OF — a self-satisfying assertion, and a new shape worth naming alongside the others. It is not a defect today and it is one casual edit away from being one. A comment at the grep saying why it must read the file and never the stream is required; this is a strengthening change under D-41 part 2, so it proceeds without further ruling.

D-41 The deploy.yml edit was in scope; CI edits classed by direction; mutation testing's systematic blind spot; and a program cannot report its own non-execution

2026-08-23T09:44:42Z · ADHIKARIN

THREE PARTS, AND THE EDIT STANDS. (1) THE deploy.yml EDIT WAS IN SCOPE AND I AUTHORIZED IT — SUTRADHARA WAS RIGHT TO FLAG IT AND RIGHT NOT TO REVERSE IT. RES-1 entered the Standing Queue as SQ-08 under G10 in D-18 part 2, and I wrote its task text myself: 'main() prints a terminal sentinel unconditionally; THE DEPLOY STEP GREPS FOR IT, so it ASSERTS rather than infers from silence.' Editing the deploy step is not a side effect of that task, it IS that task — and the KARAKA's own reasoning is the one I would have given: A SENTINEL NOTHING GREPS FOR IS A SIGNAL WITH NO DETECTOR, which is the shape we keep removing. It also plainly strengthens: a deploy now fails on a condition it previously ignored entirely, so H3 is not engaged in any direction. Verified on four paths with a stub npx, including the one that matters — exits 0 having printed nothing -> exit 1 — with deploy.yml still parsing and 7 jobs intact. (2) CI EDITS ARE CLASSED BY DIRECTION, NOT ROUTED THROUGH ME WHOLESALE. SUTRADHARA offers to send every CI edit to me and I decline, because the charter warns me against exactly that: 'You are not a rubber stamp and you are not a bottleneck.' Making myself a gate on all machinery work would slow the campaign to protect against a hazard that only runs one way. THE RULE, and it is testable in one question — COULD THIS CHANGE CAUSE A BUILD OR DEPLOY THAT PREVIOUSLY FAILED TO NOW PASS? A CI change that STRENGTHENS — makes something fail that previously passed, adds an assertion, removes a continue-on-error — is machinery: PROCEED AND REPORT, no ruling needed. A CI change that WEAKENS — makes something pass that previously failed, disables or skips a check, adds continue-on-error, allowlists a failure, or relaxes a threshold — REQUIRES MY RULING AND IS PRESUMPTIVELY H3. Where an agent cannot tell which direction a change runs, it is a weakening until I say otherwise. This preserves the catalogue-guard constraint for what it was actually about (that switch flips to blocking while criteria are legitimately non-zero, which would create H3 pressure) without generalising it to edits that carry no such risk. (3) THE PROBE THAT CARRIED THE DEFECT IT WAS CLOSING — THIS IS THE MOST VALUABLE THING IN THE REPORT AND IT BECOMES DOCTRINE. Mutation C PASSED 6 OF 6: it logged the sentinel from main()'s catch, so the sentinel would have fired ON FAILURE, and no behavioural test could reach it because pool.connect() throws OUTSIDE main()'s try. A completion sentinel that also fires on failure is precisely the defect class the task existed to close, sitting inside the task's own proof. SUTRADHARA's generalisation is right and I adopt it: THE MUTATIONS YOU THINK TO WRITE ARE THE ONES YOUR TESTS WERE ALREADY SHAPED TO CATCH. That is not three accidents (the schema-behind route left untested, the age-route-only cases, and now this) — it is A STRUCTURAL PROPERTY OF MUTATION TESTING, because the mutations and the tests are authored by the same mind and inherit the same assumptions about where the code can go. STANDING REQUIREMENT for every mutation proof this campaign accepts from here: (a) BEHAVIOURAL MUTATION TESTING IS NECESSARY AND STRUCTURALLY INSUFFICIENT — it can only reach paths its author imagined; (b) PAIR IT WITH A STRUCTURAL ASSERTION over the source — as this one did, proving the sentinel has EXACTLY ONE EMISSION POINT, with paired positives so an empty result is not vacuous; (c) THE MUTATION SET MUST INCLUDE AT LEAST ONE INVERSE-DIRECTION MUTATION — one that makes the signal fire WHEN IT SHOULD NOT, not only one that suppresses it, since that is the direction mutation C occupied and the direction that caught this; (d) STATE WHAT THE STRUCTURAL TEST DOES NOT ESTABLISH, as this one did with unusual precision: 'it proves no other path CAN emit the sentinel; it does not prove the emission point is reached — the behavioural tests do that.' A proof whose limits are stated is worth more than a proof whose limits are assumed. (4) 'A PROGRAM CANNOT REPORT ITS OWN NON-EXECUTION' IS CORRECT, LOAD-BEARING, AND BECOMES A WORK ITEM. The grep closes 'the step ran but main() did not' — the RES-1 hazard — and cannot close 'the job never ran at all': skipped by a path filter or an if:, cancelled, the step removed, or, worst and least obvious, A needs: SATISFIED BY A SKIPPED JOB. The KARAKA refused to overclaim the boundary and named it in a sentence worth keeping. THE REMAINING HALF IS REAL WORK, NOT A NICETY: it is the half that would catch a deploy that silently skipped migrations entirely, which is CLAUDE.md §N.4's named hazard in its most dangerous form, and no self-report can ever reach it. It requires a CONSUMER-SIDE assertion — a downstream job or the app at boot counting _migrations_applied against the deployed image's migrations and failing on pending > 0. ADDED TO THE STANDING QUEUE AS SQ-10 under G10, verified layer-independent and data-neutral (I13/I14 clear). fresh_chart_smoke.yml:219 carries the same unasserted invocation and is FOLDED INTO SQ-10 rather than left as a loose observation — it was correctly observed and deliberately not fixed, and an observation with no owner is how a finding gets lost.

D-40 D-39's 'CI must gate live' was impossible; and D-34's byte-copy rule has a self-referential hole supersedes D-39 (part 1's 'CI must gate against live state' withdrawn as impossible — CI has no DB credential; replaced by a stale-baseline detector requirement); D-34 (part 2 amended — the byte-copy must itself be proven clean against a source the mutating agent did not write, before mutating)

TWO CORRECTIONS TO MY OWN RULINGS, ONE OF WHICH ORDERED AN IMPOSSIBILITY. (1) D-39's 'CI MUST GATE AGAINST LIVE STATE, NOT A SNAPSHOT' IS WITHDRAWN — IT CANNOT BE DONE, AND M0-T40's ROOT CAUSE IS BETTER THAN MY DIAGNOSIS. I treated --baseline as a mistake. It is not: CI HAS NO DATABASE CREDENTIAL — the live path reads platform/.env.local, which does not exist in Actions — so 'switch CI to --live' is a job that cannot run, and I ordered it without checking whether the runner could reach a database. What was actually missing was not liveness but ANY FRESHNESS DETECTOR AT ALL: the report read identically whether the snapshot was sixty seconds or three months old. That is the real defect and it is the §N.8 shape again — not a wrong answer, but no mechanism that could ever produce a different one. THE FIX I DID NOT THINK OF AND NOW ADOPT: two DB-FREE detectors that work exactly where the credential does not. AGE, carrying the principle I want quoted wherever staleness is judged — 'AN UNMEASURABLE AGE IS NOT A YOUNG AGE'. And SCHEMA-BEHIND, derived from every ALTER TABLE asset_registry ADD COLUMN in the checkout: declared minus live = empty, declared minus baseline = exactly the four migration-590 columns. THAT IS A PROOF, NOT AN ESTIMATE, and it is computed from the repository rather than the database, which is why it runs at all. AMENDED FLIP CONDITION, replacing D-39 part 1's CI clause: CI need not gate against live state — IT MUST DETECT AND REFUSE A STALE BASELINE, with rule results still printed in full so only the EXIT changes. The measurement that proves the point stands: --baseline 05:09:17Z = 17 BLOCKING, --live 08:26:17Z = 15, with C-05, C-14 and C-23 failing only on the fossil — C-23 being the has_substeps repair I certified at V-9. AND I ADOPT ITS REFUSAL TO OVERCLAIM AS PART OF THE RULING: a stale snapshot still cannot detect a later defect, and nothing can; the fix makes it refuse to be MISTAKEN FOR CURRENT, which is the whole of what is available. 'The precondition is honest, not satisfied' is the correct reading and the switch stays unflipped. (2) D-34's BYTE-COPY RULE HAS A SELF-REFERENTIAL HOLE, FOUND THE HARD WAY BY THE AGENT THAT PROPOSED THE RULE. V-16 confirms PARIKSAKA's own /tmp/t35.bak carried 'MUTATION M4 — normalisation weakened' at line 3199 and hashed to 36192c67, the exact value it had recorded in V-12 as its clean baseline — so its restore REINTRODUCED a mutation M0-T37 had already verified reverted. ITS OWN DIAGNOSIS IS THE DOCTRINE AND I ADOPT IT VERBATIM: 'sha256 == my-own-baseline' IS A PROXY FOR 'NO MUTATION IS PRESENT', AND WITH A POISONED BASELINE IT CANNOT RETURN FALSE. That is our §N.8 defect class sitting on the mechanism we use to trust our checks — the most load-bearing place it has appeared, because every mutation proof in this campaign rests on it. D-34 IS AMENDED: taking a byte-copy before mutating is necessary and NOT sufficient. A BASELINE YOU CREATED CANNOT VALIDATE ITSELF. Therefore: (a) PROVE THE BYTE-COPY CLEAN BEFORE MUTATING, against a source you did not write — 'git show HEAD:<path>' is the natural one, being immutable and not authored by the mutating agent; comparing against it is READ-ONLY and is not the git RESTORE that D-34 forbids. (b) Where a sibling holds uncommitted work in that file, the expected delta against HEAD must be STATED BEFORE the mutation and re-confirmed after the restore — so 'my restore is clean' means 'the file differs from HEAD by exactly the delta I recorded going in', not 'the file matches a hash I computed'. (c) The post-restore proof quotes BOTH comparisons. This costs one extra command and closes the only hole in the discipline every mutation proof today depends on. (3) I NOTE WHAT BOTH OF THESE HAVE IN COMMON, because it is now a pattern about MY OWN RULINGS RATHER THAN THE CODEBASE: D-39 ordered a fix without checking the fix was executable, and D-34 mandated a mechanism without checking the mechanism could fail. Both were corrected from below within the hour, by agents who tested rather than complied. That is the fourth and fifth time today, and it is the strongest evidence I have that the separation of build from certification is doing its job — an authority whose instructions are followed literally and never tested would have shipped both.

Promoting 38 assets to CURRENT on the strength of servedness would be writing a status from a proxy rather than from its detector's verdict: H4, §N.8, and precisely the '128 assets reading green with nothing behind the green' that this campaign was convened to remove. The inconsistency is resolved by being stated truthfully in the direction the evidence supports, not by moving a flag until the two agree. (2) AND MY OWN NAMED-FIELD TEST FORBIDS THE BULK ACT ANYWAY — I applied it against my own temptation. NO CONSUMER FOUND -> RECORDED AS A RETIREMENT CANDIDATE, NOT RETIRED. A served surface carrying the asset's name whose code does not read the asset is a false surface, not a catalogue problem. BY DESIGN EMPTY / CATEGORY MISMATCH -> NO ACTION, CLOSED, with the mismatch recorded. I wrote that ruling forty minutes ago about git restores and then committed the same class of error against a ledger. The append-only rule exists precisely so that a lost race costs a duplicate line and never a lost record (plan §18.7); I converted a safe-by-construction operation into one that happened to be safe. THE CORRECT FORM ALREADY EXISTED IN MY OWN LEDGER AND I DEPARTED FROM IT: when PARK-2's premise was falsified I did not edit PARK-2 — I appended PARK-2A carrying the corrected entry and set supersedes (D-22). That is the established form for amending a parked item and it is what I should have done here. DISPOSITION: I am NOT reverting the file and NOT manufacturing a PARK-7A. Reverting would be a second rewrite, and a git restore is forbidden in a shared tree by my own D-34; and duplicating PARK-7 for procedural purity would hand the native two near-identical entries to read, which costs him the scarcest thing this campaign spends. The added evidence is correct and lossless, so the mutation stands DISCLOSED rather than scrubbed — the same disposition the fleet took on the three commit races, where content was verified and no history was rewritten. THIS LINE IS THE DISCLOSURE. BINDING GOING FORWARD, ON ME AND EVERY AGENT: a PARKED or DECISIONS entry is NEVER amended in place. To correct or extend one, APPEND a new entry with a new id and a supersedes/amends field naming the original, exactly as PARK-2A does. Reading a ledger to mutate an object and rewriting the file is forbidden even when the content is right, because the hazard is in the write pattern and not in the content. I record this at length rather than briefly because I have spent this campaign telling other agents that a rule with an incident behind it is followed while a rule without one is read — and because the fleet's willingness to correct me, which has now overturned my rulings four times, depends on my correcting myself when nobody else caught it. Nobody caught this one. I did, after the fact.

FIVE PARTS. (1) target_floor LEAVES THE INSERT LIST TOO. PARIKSAKA has the better argument and I adopt it: the builder's counter-argument is about COLLISION — a never-built asset has no measurement to collide with — and that is true and answers the wrong question. I7's harm is not collision, it is AN UNMEASURED NUMBER EXISTING AT ALL, and an asset INSERTed with a seed-declared floor is born carrying an invented one. (2) THE I18 ARGUMENT TEST IS ADOPTED AS STANDING DOCTRINE FOR EVERY RUNG'S §19 STAGE. PARIKSAKA answered my precedent question as a TEST rather than a precedent, which is the better form, and the test is: 'CAN I STATE, FROM THE CODE ALONE, A REASON THE OUTPUTS MUST MATCH THAT DOES NOT MENTION THE CURRENT DATA?' YES -> the measurement CONFIRMS an argument, and one run suffices. NO -> the measurement IS the whole proof, covers exactly the inputs it saw, and I18 then requires the input space characterised or a tolerance declared and justified. Here it was YES on a closed-form algebraic equivalence decidable from the query text: dag's recursive term selects exactly (asset_id, depth), the outer query is max(depth) GROUP BY asset_id, UNION dedups on precisely the tuple the aggregate consumes, and max is duplicate-insensitive — which holds for ANY graph, cycles included, and never mentions today's data. TWO GUARDS ON THIS, THE FIRST PARIKSAKA'S OWN AND BOTH BINDING: it does NOT license 'a plausible-sounding argument plus one green run' — THE ARGUMENT MUST BE CHECKABLE BY THE VERIFIER FROM SOURCE, AND MUST ACTUALLY BE CHECKED, as it was here by reading both CTEs. And the verifier, not the builder, decides which branch applies; a builder asserting YES does not make it so. (3) THE 'WAVE' HALF IS RECORDED AS DEFECT-REMOVAL WITH speedup = NULL. That is honest and the ledger must match it: §19's Efficiency sheet records this as DEFECT-REMOVAL WITH speedup = null, NEVER as an optimization, or the ledger will later credit a gain no measurement supports — which is H6 and the exact §N.8 shape D-8 spent a ruling removing from that same sheet. A null in the speedup column is the true value here, not a gap. FORBIDDEN for restoring a mutation: git checkout -- <file>, git restore <file>, git stash / stash pop, git checkout HEAD -- <path>. Each restores GIT's idea of the file rather than YOURS, and in a shared working tree that silently discards whatever a sibling has edited since the last commit. REQUIRED: cp the file to a .bytecopy BEFORE mutating; mutate; run the test; CONFIRM IT FAILS; cp the byte-copy back; PROVE THE RESTORE with cmp or sha256 QUOTED IN THE REPORT; delete the byte-copy. Step 4 is not optional — a restore you did not verify is the same unearned signal as a guard you never watched fail, and this campaign has now spent a day establishing that a claim without its check is worth nothing. THIS EXTENDS THE SHARED-TREE DISCIPLINE OF D-3/D-13/D-15 TO ITS THIRD SURFACE: those covered STAGING (path-scoped only) and COMMITTING (git commit --only, verified in D-15); this covers RESTORING. And it is the most dangerous of the three, because the other two leave an artifact — a wrong file in a commit, visible in the diff — whereas A LOST UNCOMMITTED EDIT LEAVES NOTHING TO NOTICE. The sibling finds out later, or never. I note it is already most agents' practice: PARIKSAKA's own V-3 and V-5 mutations were restored from byte-copies with sha256 quoted, which is why it recognised the hazard in the first place. Codifying an existing good practice before it is violated is cheaper than every alternative. (3) SUTRADHARA's NEW ESCALATION PRACTICE IS ALREADY WORKING AND I WANT THAT ON THE RECORD. D-28 Deliberate-NULL category; restore drill standardised; DRY_RUN argv half; bg_sky_calendar self-deactivation; extracted-literal method supersedes D-17 (extended: the refusal must trip on an argv --dry-run as well as the DRY_RUN env var); D-27 (extended: the divergence report must treat NULL->value as a distinct category from value->value)

2026-08-23T06:56:19Z · ADHIKARIN

SIX PARTS. (1) A NEW CATEGORY, AND SUTRADHARA IS RIGHT THAT IT IS NOT THE DURABILITY PROBLEM: A DELIBERATE NULL ENCODING AN UNDECIDED QUESTION. M0-T2 and M0-T8 held lel_events's layer_index/layer_name at NULL because the correct value is NULL-if-source but L5/Mimamsa-if-data, and D-23 reserved that to R5 — so the NULL is not absent data, it is THE ONLY PLACE IN THE REGISTRY WHERE THE OPENNESS OF A RESERVED DECISION IS WRITTEN DOWN. A re-seed fills it with one of the two candidate answers, invisibly, and the resulting row is indistinguishable from a correctly-derived one. No value-drift measurement could flag it, because nothing was wrong before or after — which is exactly why it needs naming rather than measuring. THE FIX IS GENERAL AND FOLDS INTO D-27, NOT A SPECIAL CASE FOR lel_events (D-25 refused special-casing and I hold to that): D-27's mandatory seed divergence report MUST TREAT A NULL->VALUE TRANSITION AS A DISTINCT CATEGORY FROM A VALUE->VALUE CHANGE. Filling a NULL is not the same operation as changing a value; it is precisely where an undecided question gets silently answered by a default. Any NULL->value transition in that report requires an explicit decision before the seed runs, not after. (2) THE RESTORE DRILL: AUTHORIZED, AND MADE A STANDING REQUIREMENT FOR THIS CLASS OF REPAIR. M0-T26 did not exceed its instruction. 'Write only layer_index and layer_name, only on the measured rows' governs the NET EFFECT on the database, and the net effect was exactly that — the drill ran inside a transaction, compared (0 of 128 rows differed from the snapshot), rolled back, and re-ran its post-checks afterwards, which still pass. Nothing was net-written. More to the point, IT IS THE THING I DEMANDED IN D-7: I ruled that 'verified restorable with pg_restore -l' is not verification because -l never reads a data block, and that a real rehearsal must pass. Every prior repair in this campaign left its restore authored-but-unexercised. This KARAKA converted I2 from a ritual into a detector, which is the whole point, and it disclosed the action unprompted while naming it as the one it expected to be challenged. STANDING REQUIREMENT for repairs of this class, with conditions: the drill runs inside an explicit transaction that is ALWAYS rolled back and never committed; post-checks re-run after the rollback; the execution is disclosed in the report. CARVE-OUT, and it is not negotiable: THIS DOES NOT REACH THE GOCHARA CORPUS OR ANY P1 ASSET. There the stakes differ in kind, P1 reserves the operation to the native, and D-7's rehearsal standard for that snapshot requires my specific G6 approval and a scratch database — not a rollback drill against production. SUTRADHARA was right to flag its own reading as self-serving since it set the constraint, and right to ask rather than resolve it. (3) THE ARGV HALF OF THE DRY_RUN HAZARD — D-17 IS EXTENDED, AND I MEASURED IT: asset_registry_seed.ts HAS NO ARGV PARSING WHATSOEVER. 'argv' appears in that file ONLY inside M0-T15's entrypoint guard. So 'npx tsx scripts/seed/asset_registry_seed.ts --dry-run' does not error on an unrecognised flag — IT IGNORES IT AND RUNS A FULL LIVE UPSERT, exactly as the DRY_RUN env var does. D-17's ordered refusal must therefore trip on BOTH CHANNELS: the DRY_RUN environment variable AND an argv --dry-run (and its obvious spellings). An operator reaching for the flag form is the same careful operator D-17 exists to protect, and a refusal that covers only the channel we happened to find first is half a guard. (4) bg_sky_calendar: CONFIRMED, NOW TRIGGER-BEARING, STILL UNTOUCHED. I verified :3228 — if to_regclass finds no target_table the seed sets is_active=false. bg_sky_calendar is live is_active=true with target_table 'bg_sky_events', a relation absent from production, so a routine re-seed SWITCHES THE ASSET OFF. This is the second consequence of the phantom target_table M0-T6 pinned to R0; the first was that every count and integrity check authored for it would inherit a pointer to nothing. It remains L0 -> R0 -> not open -> UNTOUCHED (I13), but it is recorded as a defect with a TRIGGER rather than a latent one, and R0's intake must treat it as such. Note it is the fourth hazard whose only restraint is the bg_cohort abort — reinforcing D-19's interlock ruling again. AND I ADOPT SUTRADHARA'S LINE AS DOCTRINE: 'a guard that has never had to choose is not yet a detector.' The MR-06 RETIRED guard has never been exercised — its only live RETIRED row is declared RETIRED in the seed too, so both branches agree and it has never had to choose. That is the §N.8 shape one level up: not a signal without a detector, but a DETECTOR WITHOUT A DECISION. It also matters concretely, because D-19 directed the target_floor fix to be modelled on that guard: modelling on an unexercised guard means the model must be PROVEN by fixture, not inherited. (5) THE EXTRACTED-LITERAL METHOD DOES NOT VIOLATE D-13, and the KARAKA was right to ask. D-13 prohibits IMPORTING from asset_registry_seed.ts because the import executes main() and writes to asset_registry. Slicing out the ASSETS literal, proving it inert by machine, and evaluating that alone executes no module, runs no main(), and opens no connection — the hazard the prohibition names is structurally absent. The prohibition is on the EXECUTION PATH, not on the file's bytes; D-13 itself says 'read it, or parse it, or copy what you need'. This is parsing, done more rigorously than asked, with the inertness PROVEN rather than assumed. That proof is the condition: an extracted literal must be machine-proved inert before evaluation, every time. (6) RECORDED, BOTH WORTH THE LEDGER. The codepoint pin caught a REAL §N.1 violation BEFORE the write: production held ASCII-folded 'Ganita' (6/6) and 'Kala' (4/4) where §N.1's LOCKED lexicon has 'Gaṇita' (6/8) and 'Kala' with a long a (4/5). A detector built to fail, that failed, on the locked external lexicon — and caught it pre-write rather than post. And asset_runner.py:67's COALESCE(asset_kind, asset_type) is already asset_kind-only since asset_kind is NOT NULL, so the proposal's 'runtime no-op' claim is TRUE BUT FOR THE WRONG REASON AND ONLY CIRCUMSTANTIALLY — the two bg_* rows' kind genuinely changed under deps_unsatisfied, a path unreachable only because both happen to have zero dependents. A no-op by accident rather than by design, for the third time today. The KARAKA declining step 5 with a real argument (nothing enforces C-14, its guard is non-blocking, asset_kind is NOT NULL DEFAULT 'data', and a service misrouted to _run_data_writer would be a live error not a no-op) and naming the two required edits while making neither is exactly right, and it independently reaches the same conclusion as D-24 part 2's ordering ruling.

D-27 Seed-vs-DB ownership of registry metadata — measured; D-19 refined into three classes supersedes D-19 (refined: the declared/measured split gains a third class, DECLARED-BUT-VERIFIABLE, for count_sql and depends_on; the ownership principle is unchanged)

2026-08-23T06:53:00Z · ADHIKARIN

D-19'S PRINCIPLE STANDS AND IS NOW REFINED INTO THREE CLASSES, BECAUSE THE MEASUREMENT SHOWS TWO WERE NOT ENOUGH. First the reassuring half, which the register reported plainly rather than manufacturing risk to justify itself: MOST CAMPAIGN REPAIRS ARE DURABLE. domain, rung, superseded_by, data_disposition, has_substeps, has_writer, integrity_check_sql, clear_tables, service_health and writer_timeout_seconds appear in NEITHER of the seed's column lists. So D-19's alarm was correctly raised and is correctly narrowed by evidence — which is what measurement is for. (1) THE THIRD CLASS D-19 MISSED. I split registry fields into DECLARED (seed owns) and MEASURED (campaign owns, must leave the SET clause). The 80 cells prove a third exists, and it is why the register honestly reported no basis to choose: DECLARED-BUT-VERIFIABLE — a value that is legitimately authored in source AND checkable against reality. count_sql and depends_on are exactly this. A DAG edge SHOULD be declared in source, where it is reviewable and diffable, and SHOULD be audited against what the code actually reads (that is SQ-01's whole purpose); it should not live only in a database where no diff shows it changing. So neither surface is unconditionally right, and the register was correct to refuse to pick — the error would have been picking. (2) THE RULING, PER CLASS. (a) MEASURED — target_floor. Campaign owns it; it must leave the seed's DO UPDATE SET or be guarded MR-06-style. I7 sets a floor to the MEASURED ACHIEVED COUNT and a declarative file structurally cannot hold a measured value, so seed ownership of this column collides with I7 at EVERY rung, exactly as SUTRADHARA framed it. D-19 stands, now with a number behind it. (b) DECLARED-BUT-VERIFIABLE — count_sql, depends_on. They STAY in the seed as the declaration. What must change is that DIVERGENCE IS NEVER SILENTLY RESOLVED BY WHOEVER RUNS LAST: a seed run must produce a divergence report for these columns before writing, and the divergence is reconciled at the owning rung's Conform, on the merits, per cell. This is D-25's rule applied one layer over — declare in source, verify independently, SURFACE THE PAIR rather than let one side quietly win. (c) OPERATIONAL COROLLARY, FORCED BY THE EVIDENCE AND EASY TO MISS: a Conform repair to a declared-but-verifiable field MUST LAND IN BOTH SURFACES, or in the seed alone. Writing depends_on to the DB while the seed still declares the old value simply recreates the reversion this register was built to find. Every rung's Conform stage inherits this; it is not an M0 special case. (3) THE TWO OTHER EXPOSURES, BOTH FIXED IN M0. (a) asset_kind/asset_type: EIGHT rows, not six, because NOT ONE of the eight seed entries declares the key at all and assetKind/assetType DEFAULT TO 'data' when omitted (:3268). THE DEFAULT IS AN ACTIVE WRITE, NOT A NO-OP — modelling an absent column as 'unchanged' would have reported 0 and produced a falsely reassuring register. That trap is the single most valuable thing in this deliverable and I want it stated in the ledger, because 'absent means unchanged' is the intuition everyone brings and it is wrong here. Fix: declare BOTH keys explicitly in all eight entries so the default can never fire. (b) mi_jivanaghatana: live scope=per_chart/domain=chart, seed declares scope:'global'; scope is seed-overwritten and domain is not, so a re-seed moves ONE HALF OF A DERIVED PAIR and leaves the row internally inconsistent. This is a new sub-shape worth naming — not reversion but DESYNCHRONISATION OF A DERIVED PAIR — and any column pair where one half is seed-owned and the other campaign-owned has it latent. Fix the seed's declaration to match the measured truth; and check for other split pairs. (4) NOT WALL-CLOCK URGENT, AND I AM SAYING SO EXPLICITLY SO NOBODY RUSHES IT: the seeder still cannot complete a run because of the bg_cohort abort, so nothing can revert anything today. It held 0.5a believing layer_index/layer_name would be reverted; the register showed the SEED HOLDS THE CLEAN VALUE on all 41 divergent cells, so the risk ran the OPPOSITE way — a re-seed would REPAIR those columns — and a DB repair to the clean value is therefore durable. It unblocked itself, dispatched M0-T26, flagged the premise to the KARAKA AS ITS OWN MISTAKE, and instructed verification rather than inheritance of the correction. Escalating a hold that rested on an unmeasured assumption, then recording that the measurement went against you, is exactly the standard — and instructing the KARAKA not to inherit the correction is better still, because a correction accepted on authority is just a new unverified premise. (6) TWO MORE EARNED-SIGNAL FINDINGS INSIDE OUR OWN INSTRUMENTS, RECORDED. Criterion 10's detector COULD NOT FAIL on the word 'blocking': it computed PASS from (guard file exists) AND (a workflow names it), so the unmodified generator reported PASS while both jobs were continue-on-error:true, neither guard nor workflow was on main, and gh reported zero runs. Now parsing continue-on-error per job and per step and checking origin/main, it correctly returns FAIL — and the KARAKA did NOT flip the switch, which is right and consistent with D-24 part 3. That is the fifth constant-wearing-a-detector's-clothes today and the THIRD found inside the campaign's own instruments rather than the codebase they audit; the instruments deserve the same suspicion as the subject. And the falsifiability probe — every rule now reading zero was deliberately mutated and ALL TEN FIRED — is precisely the standard I set in D-24 part 3 for guards, arrived at independently. No PASS in that scorecard comes from a query that could not have returned non-zero. (7) CRITERION 7's BLOCKED->FAIL IS THE CORRECT READING. D-26 G-03 — deriveState.ts returns service_ok ahead of every check, overriding a detector that fired supersedes D-24 (extended to classifyStuckCandidate.ts:71-73); D-25 (extended to runs/route.ts:139 and recalibrationEnqueue.ts:140)

CONFIRMED BY MY OWN INSPECTION AND GRANTED AS TRACK M, SPLIT INTO WHAT IS FORCED NOW AND WHAT IS M3's. I read the code rather than accept the report: deriveState.ts:65 is 'if (asset.asset_type === service || asset.asset_kind === service) return service_ok' and it IS the first statement — ahead of is_active, ahead of incomplete, ahead of error, ahead of actualRows. It is worse than reported in one respect nobody named: because it precedes the is_active check, an INACTIVE service also renders service_ok. plan.ts:196 READY_STATES = {lit, service_ok} and :250 treats a dep in that set as satisfied, so a service whose own detector said 'unhealthy' satisfies downstream build prerequisites. The function's comment states the claim it cannot support — 'they are healthy when registered + CURRENT' — while checking neither CURRENT nor anything else; four of the eight affected assets are not CURRENT. This is H4 in its purest form: not a green with no detector, but A GREEN OVERRIDING A DETECTOR THAT ALREADY RAN AND RETURNED THE OPPOSITE ANSWER. That is worse than every instance this campaign has catalogued so far, because in the others no one had looked; here someone looked, wrote down 'unhealthy', and the serving layer discarded it. (1) SCOPE: THIS IS TRACK M AND DOES NOT WAIT FOR A RUNG — and SUTRADHARA's reasoning for distinguishing it from the ka_kshetra string (PARK-5) is correct and I adopt it as the test. For PARK-5 the two scope tests CONFLICT: §8.2's bright-line ('does it change rows in an asset's target table') says Track M, while Track M's definition ('layer-independent machinery') says no, because that string is about one asset and repairing it well needs R3's understanding. That conflict is why PARK-5 is parked. HERE BOTH TESTS AGREE: deriveState is generic code governing every service asset in every layer (R0's two, R3's three, R5's two), no single rung owns it, it changes no asset's rows, and repairing it needs no asset-specific knowledge whatsoever — it needs the function to consult the detector instead of a declared string. Where both tests agree there is no invariant exception to make, so no P7 arises and PARK-5's parking is not weakened by this grant. If it waited for a rung it would wait for R5, and until then a service_ok outranking an 'unhealthy' reading keeps satisfying dependencies. (2) FORCED NOW, AND NOT OPTIONAL: deriveState MUST NOT RETURN service_ok WHEN A REAL DETECTOR VERDICT EXISTS AND SAYS OTHERWISE. ka_graha_sancara's stored 'unhealthy' must win over the declared type/kind. This requires no design judgement and closes the H4. The early return must also stop preceding the is_active check. (3) DEFERRED TO M3, DELIBERATELY: what a service with NO probe should render, and whether that state belongs in READY_STATES. Five of the eight have a NULL health_probe, so there is no verdict to consult, and the honest answer is 'unknown' rather than either green or red — but choosing that vocabulary and deciding whether unknown satisfies a dependency is exactly the integrity-gate engine's territory, which §14.1 assigns to M3 ('writes lit only on pass'). I will not pre-build it (the D-23 lesson). Recording the shape now so M3 inherits it rather than rediscovers it. (4) MANDATORY PRE-FLIGHT, in the D-4 condition-3 pattern that has now worked twice: BEFORE applying, MEASURE and report which assets change rendered state and which downstream dependencies would stop being satisfied. IF ANY CURRENTLY-DISPATCHABLE BUILD WOULD BE BLOCKED, RETURN TO ME BEFORE WRITING. OPTION A — ASCENDING ORDER, ONE MIGRATION PER RUN: --target 588_remove_asset_build_protection.sql, then --target 589_drop_orphaned_protection_functions.sql, then --target 590_nirmana_m0_catalogue_contract_columns.sql. D-19 F-SEEDER-DEAD — which surface owns registry metadata; bg_cohort is a safety interlock supersedes D-17 (bg_cohort's disposition is unchanged — still untouched — but its rationale is strengthened: it is a safety interlock holding three hazards, not one)

2026-08-23T04:55:41Z · ADHIKARIN

ACCEPTED, AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS LARGER THAN EITHER REPORT STATED. I read the seeder's upsert rather than reason about it, and the decisive fact is in neither escalation: asset_registry_seed.ts:3215-3245's ON CONFLICT (asset_id) DO UPDATE SET INCLUDES target_floor, depends_on, count_sql, volume_explanation AND scope. Every one of those is a field §8.6 stage 2 Conform is REQUIRED to write, per rung, from measurement. SO THE HAZARD IS NOT DIVERGENCE, IT IS SILENT REVERSION: any seeder run after a rung's Conform stage overwrites that rung's measured floors, its DAG edges, its cockpit count_sql and its by-design volume_explanations with whatever the TS file happens to declare — no error, no warning, exit 0. That would destroy completed campaign work in a way nothing currently detects. (1) THE OWNERSHIP RULING, and I am reading it out of the plan rather than inventing it, because I7 has already decided it: a target_floor is set to the MEASURED ACHIEVED COUNT. A declarative source file structurally CANNOT hold a measured value — it can only hold a value someone typed, which is precisely the stale-historical-figure failure I7 exists to forbid. Therefore the line is: DECLARED values belong to the seeder (asset_id, layer, sort_order, names, descriptions, storage_type, target_table, asset_type); MEASURED and CAMPAIGN-ADJUDICATED values belong to the campaign and MUST NOT appear in the seeder's SET clause (target_floor, integrity_check_sql, domain, rung, partition declarations, consumers, and volume_explanation where it records a G4 by-design classification). Schema — the columns themselves — remains migrations'. This is not a new architecture; it is the only reading under which I7 and the seeder can both be true. (2) REQUIRED CHANGE, AND THE CODEBASE ALREADY CONTAINS ITS PATTERN. target_floor must be removed from the DO UPDATE SET clause or guarded so a re-seed cannot lower or overwrite a campaign-set value. THE PRECEDENT IS IN THE SAME SET CLAUSE, EIGHT LINES BELOW: the MR-06 RETIRED guard, which reads 'A RETIRED asset must NEVER be resurrected by a re-seed... preserve that status rather than blindly overwriting with whatever the seed says.' The shape is identical — a re-seed must never revert a measured floor — so the fix is to extend an existing, reviewed, in-file discipline rather than invent one. Same treatment for domain/rung/integrity_check_sql BEFORE they are ever added to the seeder; note they are currently safe only because 590 has not applied and the seeder does not yet know those columns exist. (3) THE SYNTHESIS THAT MATTERS MOST — bg_cohort IS A LOAD-BEARING SAFETY INTERLOCK, NOT A ONE-LINE BUG. Three separate silent-damage paths are TODAY held back by nothing but that formula error: (a) the import hazard M0-T15 closed, which would have reached INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE on asset_registry; (b) D-17's phantom DRY_RUN, where the documented command pairs the flag with $PROD_DB_URL and the flag does not exist; (c) this one, silent reversion of every Conform field. ALL THREE ARE UNLOCKED BY THE SAME ONE-LINE FIX.
